  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that the fermionic Laughlin <i>V</i><sub>1</sub> Hamiltonian at filling 1/3 on expanding round spheres retains a unique ground state and a uniform spectral gap under sufficiently weak bounded real scalar one-body potentials projected to the lowest Landau level. With coefficient one for each pair projector and Laughlin flux $`q=3(N-1)`$, the gap and perturbation threshold are uniform for all sufficiently large particle numbers and all potential profiles of supremum norm at most one. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the fermionic Laughlin <i>V</i><sub>1</sub> Hamiltonian at filling 1/3 on expanding round spheres retains a unique ground state and a uniform spectral gap under sufficiently weak bounded real scalar one-body potentials projected to the lowest Landau level. With coefficient one for each pair projector and Laughlin flux $`q=3(N-1)`$, the gap and perturbation threshold are uniform for all sufficiently large particle numbers and all potential profiles of supremum norm at most one. The result uses the uniform unperturbed Fock-space gap and gives existential constants.

- **claim-main-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are constants $\lambda_*>0$, $\Delta_*>0$, and $N_*<\infty$ such that, for every $N\ge N_*$, every real bounded measurable $\varphi$ on $S_{3(N-1)}$ with $\\|\varphi\\|_\infty\le1$, and every real $\|\lambda\|\le\lambda_*$, $$\label{eq:intro-stability} E_1(N,\lambda,\varphi)-E_0(N,\lambda,\varphi)\ge\Delta_*.$$ In particular the perturbed ground state is unique.
  - Explanation: Under weak bounded scalar potentials the gap above the perturbed ground state stays bounded below uniformly in particle number and in the potential profile, so the ground state remains unique.
- **claim-zero-mode-polynomials** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-zero-modes)): For $n\ge1$, a vector lies in $Z_{n,q}$ if and only if its polynomial has the form $$\label{eq:zero-polynomial} \prod_{i<j}(z_{i0}z_{j1}-z_{i1}z_{j0})^3\,S(z_1,\ldots,z_n),$$ where $S$ is symmetric and homogeneous of degree $h=q+3-3n$ in each spinor. Consequently $Z_{n,q}=\{0\}$ when $h<0$, and, when $h\ge0$, $$\dim Z_{n,q}=\binom{n+h}{n}.$$ At $q=3(N-1)$ the space $Z_{N,q}$ is the Laughlin line, and every nonempty zero sector has particle number at most $N$. The vacuum sector $Z_{0,q}$ has dimension one.
  - Explanation: Zero modes are exactly the polynomials carrying the cube of every pair bracket times a symmetric factor, which gives the dimension count and the structure of the Laughlin line.
- **claim-unperturbed-gap** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-unperturbed)): Let $\gamma_*=4616733319001/10^{14}$. For every $0<\gamma<\gamma_*$ there is $q_\gamma$ such that $$\label{eq:accepted-fock-gap} H_q^2\ge\gamma H_q\qquad(q\ge q_\gamma)$$ on all of $\mathcal F_q$. In particular, for all sufficiently large $N$ and $q=3(N-1)$, $$\label{eq:accepted-filled-gap} H_{N,q}\ge\frac1{25}(I-P_{N,q}).$$
  - Explanation: The unperturbed pair Hamiltonian satisfies a coercivity inequality on the whole Fock space for large flux, imported from a companion manuscript, and this gives the filled-sector gap.
- **claim-frame-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-frame)): For every $D$ there is $C_D$ such that $$\|\langle z_x,k_y\rangle\|\leq C_D(1+d(x,y))^{-D}. \label{eq:frame-coherent-decay}$$ For two frames constructed as above, possibly with different axes, their atoms satisfy the same bound with $k_y$ replaced by an atom centered at $y$. For each fixed integer $J$, <a href="#eq:frame-coherent-decay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:frame-coherent-decay">[eq:frame-coherent-decay]</a> also holds, with constants depending on $J$, for every rotated polar orbital $e_j(y)$ with $0\leq j\leq J$.
  - Explanation: Frame atoms have overlap with coherent vectors decaying faster than any power of the distance.
- **claim-common-frames** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-common-frames)): For each fixed $d_0$, frames at fluxes $q$ and $t$ with $0\leq q-t\leq d_0$ may be embedded in the same slots so that index identification $i_{q,t}:e_j^{(t)}\mapsto e_j^{(q)}$, $j\leq t$, satisfies the exact identity $\mathsf S_t=\mathsf S_q i_{q,t}$. All preceding estimates remain uniform. In the northern third the windows can be chosen from one system independent of the flux; there one may use the common metric with radial variable $\sqrt{1+m}$.
  - Explanation: Frames at nearby fluxes can share slots so the smaller analysis map is the larger one composed with the index identification.
- **claim-core-shells** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-core-shells)): If <a href="#eq:local-core-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-core-tail">[eq:local-core-tail]</a> holds with $r\geq1$, there is a decomposition $A=\sum_{\nu\geq0}A_\nu$ with $A_\nu\in\mathfrak A(B_{C_*2^{\nu+1}r}(x))$ and $\\|A_\nu\\|\leq C_Da2^{-\nu D}$ for every $D$. Consequently, for each fixed $B\geq0$, $$\sum_{\nu\geq0}(C_*2^{\nu+1}r)^B\\|A_\nu\\| \leq C_Ba r^B. \label{eq:local-core-moment}$$
  - Explanation: An operator with a core radius and fast tails splits into dyadic shells with rapidly decaying norms, giving weighted moment bounds.
- **claim-local-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-calculus)): The following bounds are uniform over the slot systems above.* 1. *Products of operators rapidly local at the same center, and adjoints, preserve rapid locality. If $A,B$ are centered at $x,y$, respectively, with bounded local norms, then $\\|[A,B]_{\mathrm{gr}}\\|\leq C_D(1+d(x,y))^{-D}$ for every $D$.* 2. *If $A$ is rapidly local at $x$ and a rapidly local interaction $K_y$ has tempered size $a(y)$, then $[\int K_y\,dy,A]_{\mathrm{gr}}$ is rapidly local at $x$, of size $a(x)$ times the local bounds of $A$.* 3. *Suppose $A$ is even, $\\|A\\|_{x,l}\leq1$, and $J_y$ has bounded rapid local norms. With $D=1+d(x,y)$ and $C_{xy}=[A,J_y]$, $$\\|C_{xy}\\|\leq C D^{-l},\qquad \\|C_{xy}\\|_{x,l}\leq C,\qquad \\|C_{xy}^*C_{xy}\\|_{x,l}\leq C D^{-l}. \label{eq:commutator-square-locality}$$* *For an operator initially supported in $B_r(x)$, the corresponding core estimates cost a fixed polynomial in $r$. At distances larger than a fixed multiple of $r$, any prescribed decay order is available.
  - Explanation: Rapidly local operators are closed under products and adjoints, graded commutators of distant operators are small, and commutators with tempered interactions stay local.
- **claim-physical-interactions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-toeplitz)): The unperturbed Hamiltonian $H_q$ and the Fock lift of any bounded real scalar symbol $\varphi$ have physical, neutral, Hermitian rapid interactions of uniform sizes $C_D$ and $C_D\\|\varphi\\|_\infty$, respectively.
  - Explanation: The pair Hamiltonian and the lifted Toeplitz perturbation both admit physical neutral Hermitian rapid interactions with uniform bounds, with no derivative of the potential entering.
- **claim-propagation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-dynamics)): Let $K$ be self-adjoint and even, with a rapidly local interaction of uniform size. Write $\tau_t^K(A)=e^{itK}Ae^{-itK}$. For each $D$ there are $D'$ and $M_D$, independent of volume, such that $$\\|\tau_t^K(A)\\|_{x,D} \leq C_D(1+\|t\|)^{M_D}\\|A\\|_{x,D'}. \label{eq:polynomial-propagation}$$ The assertion holds also for time-dependent generators with the same uniform bounds. In particular evolution over a bounded parameter interval preserves rapid locality. If $A$ is supported in $B_r(x)$, the error in approximating $\tau_t^K(A)$ on $B_R(x)$, for $R\geq C(r+(1+\|t\|)^4)$, is bounded by $C_D\\|A\\|(1+r)^2R^{-D}$.
  - Explanation: Time evolution under a rapidly local even generator preserves rapid locality with polynomial growth in time, independently of volume.
- **claim-time-filters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-filters)): Suppose $F\in L^1(\mathbb R)$ and $\int(1+\|t\|)^j\|F(t)\|\,dt<\infty$ for every $j$. Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#locality:dynamics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="locality:dynamics">9</a>, $$\mathcal T_{F,K}(A)=\int_{\mathbb R}F(t)\tau_t^K(A)\,dt \label{eq:time-filter}$$ is rapidly local whenever $A$ is, with uniform bounds. The same is true for a finite measure consisting of such a function and a mass at zero. Number grade and physicality are preserved when $K$ is neutral and physical. Suitable symmetry of $F$ preserves adjoints.
  - Explanation: Averaging the evolved observable against a kernel with all moments keeps it rapidly local and preserves grade and physicality.
- **claim-weighted-differences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-difference)): Let $K,K'$ satisfy Lemma <a href="#locality:dynamics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="locality:dynamics">9</a> uniformly, and assume $K-K'$ has a rapidly local interaction of tempered size $a(z)$. For a rapidly local $A$ centered at $x$, $$\\|\tau_t^K(A)-\tau_t^{K'}(A)\\|_{x,D} \leq C_Da(x)(1+\|t\|)^{M_D}\\|A\\|_{x,D'}. \label{eq:weighted-evolution-difference}$$ The same conclusion holds after an all-moments time filter, and for differences of bounded-parameter evolutions. If the input itself changes by local size $b(x)$, its separate contribution is $Cb(x)$. All estimates retain the product of a local input size and $a(x)$. If $A\in\mathfrak A(B_r(x))$, put $s=\kappa+2$, with $\kappa$ from <a href="#eq:tempered-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tempered-weight">[eq:tempered-weight]</a>. The difference has operator norm at most $$C a(x)\\|A\\|(1+r)^s\|t\|(1+\|t\|)^{4s}. \label{eq:difference-core}$$ For $R\geq C_*(1+r)$, with a fixed geometric constant $C_*$, it has an approximation supported in $B_{r+R}(x)$ with error at most $$C_Da(x)\\|A\\|(1+r)^s (1+\|t\|)^{4D+4s+1}(1+R)^{-D}. \label{eq:difference-tail}$$ Thus the power of the initial radius is independent of the tail order. If the differing interaction vanishes throughout a ball of radius $d$ about $x$, the bounds for a fixed rapidly local input gain arbitrary inverse powers of $1+d$.
  - Explanation: The difference of two evolutions is controlled by the local size of the difference of generators, via Duhamel and the propagation bound.
- **claim-compression-functional-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-functional-calculus)): The map <a href="#eq:physical-compression-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-compression-map">[eq:physical-compression-map]</a>, for the frames of Lemma <a href="#locality:frame" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="locality:frame">4</a>, preserves rapid locality. An operator supported in $B_r(x)$ remains localized about that ball, with polynomial core costs and arbitrary inverse-power tails outside $B_{Cr}(x)$. If $A=A^*$ is bounded and rapidly local, and $f$ is smooth on a neighborhood of its spectrum, then $f(A)$ is rapidly local. The constants depend on finitely many local bounds and derivatives for each requested decay order.
  - Explanation: Physical compression, frame changes and smooth functions of a local self-adjoint operator all preserve rapid locality.
- **claim-pair-row-symbols** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-row-symbols)): With $m=b/2$ and $w=w_q(m)$, the zero-extended coefficients satisfy $$\|\Delta_i^r\Delta_b^s a_b(i)\| \leq C_{D,r,s}w^{-1/2-r-s} \left(1+\frac{\|i-m\|}{w}\right)^{-D}. \label{eq:pair-row-symbols}$$ There are pair annihilators $L_{\nu,\phi}$, rapidly local with uniform bounds about centers $x_{\nu,\phi}$, and a measure $d\mu$ of bounded mass per unit ball, such that $$H_q=\sum_\nu\int_0^{2\pi} L_{\nu,\phi}^*L_{\nu,\phi}\,d\mu_\nu(\phi). \label{eq:pair-local-resolution}$$ The construction is linear in the row coefficients. In particular symbol bounds <a href="#eq:pair-row-symbols" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pair-row-symbols">[eq:pair-row-symbols]</a> with an additional tempered factor give local bounds with that factor; the same holds for differences of row families in common frames.
  - Explanation: Pair coefficients obey scaled difference bounds, and the Hamiltonian is an integral of squares of rapidly local pair annihilators.
- **claim-diagonal-symbols** (theorem, [source](#stmt-locality-one-body-symbol)): Suppose $A(m)>0$ is tempered with exponent $\kappa$ and $\mathfrak s_K(a;m)\leq C_KA(m)$ for every integer $K\geq0$. Then $D_q(a)=\sum_j a(j)c_j^*c_j$ has a physical, neutral, Hermitian interaction of size $A(m)$. For each output approximation order $D$, only the seminorms through $K_D=\lceil4D+4\kappa+40\rceil$ are needed. The construction and the estimate are linear in the symbol, so the same assertion holds for differences of symbols.
  - Explanation: A diagonal one-body operator whose symbol has tempered scaled derivatives has a physical neutral Hermitian local interaction of the same size.
- **claim-pinning-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pinning-uniform-gap)): There exist $q_{\mathrm{pin}}$ and $\gamma_{\mathrm{pin}}>0$ such that $$H_{q,d}(s)\ge\gamma_{\mathrm{pin}}(1-P_{q,d}(s)) \quad(q\ge q_{\mathrm{pin}},\ d\in\{1,3\},\ 0\le s\le1)$$ on the entire Fock space $\mathcal F_{q-d}$.
  - Explanation: The spectral gap persists uniformly along the pinning deformation on the whole Fock space.
- **claim-pinning-transport** (construction, [source](#stmt-pinning-generator)): There is a number-preserving and axially invariant unitary path $U_{q,d}(s)$ on $\mathcal F_t$, starting at the identity, such that $$U_{q,d}(s)\ker H_t=\ker H_{q,d}(s).$$ Writing $\partial_sU_{q,d}(s)=Y_{q,d}(s)U_{q,d}(s)$, the anti-Hermitian generator $Y_{q,d}(s)$ is a rapid interaction of size $C\alpha_q(m)$. In common frames, a bounded change in $q$, with $d$ fixed, changes the generator interaction by size $C\alpha_q(m)/v$.
  - Explanation: A number-preserving axially invariant unitary path carries the unpinned zero space onto the pinned one, generated by a rapid interaction of explicit local size.
- **claim-northern-observable** (bound, [source](#stmt-pinning-northern-observable)): Let $O$ be a neutral operator of norm at most one supported on $B_r(x)$, where $r\ge2$ and $x$ has latitude index $m$. There are fixed constants $b,C_*$, and $c=8$, independent of every tail order below, such that, if $q>Cr^b(1+m)$, the operator $$\Delta_{q,d}(O)=U_{q,d}(1)^*OU_{q,d}(1)-O$$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \\|\Delta_{q,d}(O)\\|&\le C r^c\frac{1+m}{q^2},\label{eq:pinning-northern-norm}\\ \inf_{A\in\mathfrak A(B_{r+R}(x))}\\|\Delta_{q,d}(O)-A\\| &\le C_D r^c\frac{1+m}{q^2}(1+R)^{-D} \quad(R\ge C_*(1+r)).\label{eq:pinning-northern-tail} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, it admits a decomposition $$\Delta_{q,d}(O)=\sum_{\nu\ge0}D_\nu,\qquad D_\nu\in\mathfrak A(B_{C_*2^\nu r}(x)),\qquad \\|D_\nu\\|\le C_D r^c\frac{1+m}{q^2}2^{-\nu D}. \label{eq:pinning-northern-shells}$$ For $\|q-q'\|\le3$, with the same $d$ and identical northern slots, $\Delta_{q,d}(O)-\Delta_{q',d}(O)$ obeys all these estimates with $q^{-2}$ replaced by $q^{-3}$. In particular, for every fixed $B$, the shell decomposition can be chosen so that $$\sum_{\nu\ge0}(C_*2^\nu r)^B\\|D_\nu\\| \le C_B r^{B+c}\frac{1+m}{q^2}, \label{eq:pinning-northern-moment}$$ and the same moment estimate holds for the flux difference with $q^{-3}$.
  - Explanation: A fixed local observable in the northern region changes only slightly under the transport, with rapid tails.
- **claim-weighted-transport** (bound, [source](#stmt-pinning-weighted-transport)): The operator $$K_{q,d}(f)=U_{q,d}(1)^*D_t(f)U_{q,d}(1)-D_t(f)$$ has a rapid interaction representation of size $CA_q(m)$. Its interaction difference under a bounded change in $q$, with $d$ fixed, has size $CA_q(m)/v$ in common frames. The pieces may be assigned to their original annular centers throughout the construction.
  - Explanation: The change of a weighted number observable under transport is a rapid interaction of the stated local size, with an extra inverse-distance factor for flux changes.
- **claim-one-sided-change** (bound, [source](#stmt-pinning-one-sided)): Writing $P_0$ for the zero projection of $H_t$, one has $$P_0K_{q,d}(f)P_0 \le C\left(\int_0^t\frac{A_q(m)}{w(m)}\,dm\right)P_0. \label{eq:pinning-one-sided}$$
  - Explanation: Compressed to the zero space, the transported convex weight has a one-sided bound gaining a width factor from cancellation of leading curvature terms.
- **claim-calibration-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-pinning-calibration-cost)): Let $q^0=3\lfloor q/3\rfloor$ and $r=q-q^0\in\{0,1,2\}$. Consider the interaction $K_{q,3}(f)$ on $\mathcal F_{q-3}$ and its common-frame comparison with $K_{q^0,3}(f)$. Embed a unit vector $\xi\in Z_{n,q^0-3}$ by the common orbital labels, or instead grow it to flux $q-3$ using the $r$ isometries $\mathcal U_{Q,1}$. The following costs are each bounded by $$C\int_0^{q-3}\frac{A_q(m)}{v(m)}\,dm: \label{eq:pinning-calibration-cost}$$ the change of interaction in the embedded state, and the change of its expectation between the embedded and grown states. The same bounds hold after selecting any common collection of source-center pieces, and after adding their absolute bounds. In particular the statement applies to the filled vector at flux $q^0-3$ and to separate northern and southern portions of the interaction.
  - Explanation: Changing the calibration state, by embedding or by growing flux, costs at most an integral of the transport interaction size divided by the end distance.
- **claim-branch-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-flags-branch-algebra)): For $q>q_{\mathrm{stop}}$, define $$\mathcal F_{n,q}=\ker(c_q\|_{Z_{n,q}}),\qquad \mathcal E_{n,q}=Z_{n,q}\ominus\mathcal F_{n,q}.$$ Then $\mathcal F_{n,q}=Z_{n,q}^{(1)}$. Contraction by $c_q$ maps $\mathcal E_{n,q}$ bijectively onto $Z_{n-1,q}^{(3)}$. If $\mathsf T_q$ denotes the polar part of $c_q\|_{Z_q}$, the maps $$J_{q,F}=\mathcal U_{q,1},\qquad J_{q,E}=\mathsf T_q^*\mathcal U_{q,3}$$ are isometries onto the two orthogonal branches. Consequently, $$Z_{n,q}\simeq Z_{n,q-1}\oplus Z_{n-1,q-3}. \label{eq:flags-two-branches}$$ The second summand is absent for $n=0$. In the first summand $h$ decreases by one; in the second it is unchanged.
  - Explanation: Each zero space splits orthogonally into a branch recording a flag and a branch removing an electron, with isometries onto the branches.
- **claim-polar-occupation-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-flags-polar)): There is $\varepsilon>0$, independent of $q$ and of the number sector, such that $$P_q n_qP_q\geq\varepsilon P_{\mathcal E_q}, \qquad \mathcal E_q=\bigoplus_n\mathcal E_{n,q}. \label{eq:flags-occupation-gap}$$ The polar map $\mathsf T_q$ and the projections onto the two branches, restricted to $Z_q$, have bounded physical representatives rapidly localized at the south pole. The representatives preserve $Z_q$ and its orthogonal complement. They have respectively the number and index grades of $c_q$ and of the identity.
  - Explanation: Occupation of the south-pole orbital has a uniform lower bound on the electron branch, so the polar map has rapidly localized representatives.
- **claim-weighted-branch-step** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-flags-weighted-step)): Fix $p\in(0,2)$, $L\geq2$, and $f(j)=f_{L,p}(j)$. In the orthogonal coordinates <a href="#eq:flags-two-branches" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flags-two-branches">[eq:flags-two-branches]</a>, $$\begin{split} (J_{q,F}\oplus J_{q,E})^*D_q(f)(J_{q,F}\oplus J_{q,E}) ={}&\begin{pmatrix} D_{q-1}(f)+K_{q,1}(f)&0\\ 0&D_{q-3}(f)+f(q)+K_{q,3}(f) \end{pmatrix}+R_q(f),\\ &\\|R_q(f)\\|\leq C_p\frac{f(q)}{(L+q)^2}. \end{split} \label{eq:flags-weighted-step}$$ The notation in the left side means the unitary whose columns are the two branch isometries. The bound includes the off-diagonal blocks. For affine $f$ the remainder is zero and $K_{q,d}(f)=0$.
  - Explanation: In branch coordinates a weighted one-body observable is block diagonal up to a scalar shift on the electron branch, plus a small remainder including off-diagonal blocks.
- **claim-northern-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-flags-northern-step)): There are fixed $c\geq0$ and $b_0\geq1$ with the following property. Let $O=O^*$ be neutral, of norm at most one, supported on a slot ball of radius $r\geq2$ centered at latitude index $m$, and put $u=1+m$. If $q>C r^{b_0}u$, identify all northern slots in the fluxes being compared. The two diagonal branch blocks of $O$ equal $$O+\mathcal D_{q,1}(O)\quad\hbox{and}\quad O+\mathcal D_{q,3}(O)$$ up to a matrix remainder of norm $C_Dq^{-D}$ for every fixed $D$. Each $\mathcal D_{q,d}(O)$ is neutral and self-adjoint. If $x$ is its original center, then, for fixed $c,C_*<\infty$ and every $D>0$, $$\begin{split} \\|\mathcal D_{q,d}(O)\\|&\leq Cr^cu/q^2,\\ \inf_{A_R\in\mathfrak A(B_R(x))} \\|\mathcal D_{q,d}(O)-A_R\\| &\leq C_Dr^cu q^{-2}R^{-D}\qquad(R\geq C_*r). \end{split} \label{eq:flags-northern-size}$$ For $\|q-q'\|\leq2$ and fixed $d$, the difference between these changes, in common northern slots, obeys the same two estimates with $q^{-2}$ replaced by $q^{-3}$. The exponent $c$ and core constant $C_*$ are independent of $D$.
  - Explanation: For observables far to the north the branch blocks differ from the observable by small local corrections, with negligible remainders.
- **claim-reference-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-flags-reference-compatibility)): Let two fluxes $q,q'$ use common northern slots, and let $s\leq\min(q,q')$. Embed a fixed vector of $\mathcal F_s$ into either system by the same orbital labels, with the orthogonal complement of the embedded one-particle space vacant. The two states have identical expectations on the algebra of common northern slots. In particular, references at fluxes with the same nearest lower filled flux agree on that algebra.
  - Explanation: Padded embeddings of a fixed state at different fluxes have identical expectations on the algebra of common northern slots.
- **claim-local-readout** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-flags-local-readout)): For every fixed $a\in(0,2)$ there are $B,C<\infty$ such that the following holds uniformly in $n,q$ and in the allowed slot frames. Let $O=O^*$ be a neutral slot observable of norm at most one, supported in a ball of radius $r\geq2$ with center of latitude index $m$. Its compression to $Z_{n,q}$ satisfies $$\pm\left( \mathcal R_{n,q}P_{n,q}OP_{n,q}\mathcal R_{n,q}^* -\operatorname{ref}_q(O)I\right) \leq C r^B W_m. \label{eq:flags-local-readout}$$ Here $P_{n,q}$ denotes the zero-space projection in the number-$n$ sector. More generally, for a convergent decomposition $A=\sum_\nu A_\nu$ into neutral self-adjoint observables supported in balls about the same center, of respective radii $r_\nu\geq2$, the right side can be replaced by $CW_m\sum_\nu r_\nu^B\\|A_\nu\\|$.
  - Explanation: The compression of a local neutral observable to a zero space deviates from its reference expectation by at most a weighted flag operator, uniformly in particle number and flux.
- **claim-one-hole-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-flags-one-hole)): Suppose $h=1$, equivalently $q=3n-2$. Let $\psi,\phi$ be any two unit vectors in $Z_{n,q}$. Let $\{O_x\}$ be a measurable family of neutral self-adjoint observables of norm at most one, supported in balls of a fixed radius $r\geq2$ about $x$, with center measure of bounded density. There are constants $C,B'<\infty$, independent of $n,q$, such that $$\int\left\| \langle\psi,O_x\psi\rangle-\langle\phi,O_x\phi\rangle \right\|\,dx\leq Cr^{B'}. \label{eq:flags-one-hole}$$ The same conclusion holds for rapidly localized families, with the right side bounded by the corresponding fixed rapid-locality seminorm. For a family whose norms are bounded by $A$, multiply the right side by $A$.
  - Explanation: In a one-hole space rotation averaging gives an area-independent bound on how much local expectations can differ between two states.
- **claim-pooled-charge** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-charge-pooled)): For each of $p=1/8$ and $p=3/2$ there are fixed $L\ge2$ and $c>0$ such that, for every flux and every nonzero zero-mode space $Z_{n,q}$, $$\label{eq:charge-pooled} \mathcal R_{n,q}\bigl(C_q(f)\mathbf1-P_{n,q}D_q(f)P_{n,q}\bigr)\mathcal R_{n,q}^* \ \ge\ c S_{L,p}.$$ Here compression to $Z_{n,q}$ is understood, and $P_{n,q}$ denotes its orthogonal projection. The constants are independent of $q,n$ and of the readout axis. When $h=0$, both sides vanish.
  - Explanation: A weighted one-body observable, compressed to a zero space and centered, dominates a positive multiple of the pooled flag weight for the two chosen exponents.
- **claim-charge-scalars** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-charge-scalars)): Fix $p\in(0,2)$ and a lower stopping flux $q_{\rm s}\ge8$. There is a function $\varepsilon_p(L)\to0$ as $L\to\infty$ with the following properties, uniformly for $q\ge q_{\rm s}$: $$\begin{aligned} \left\|C_{q-1}(f)-C_q(f)+\frac13f(q)\right\| &\le\varepsilon_p(L)f(q),\label{eq:charge-F-scalar}\\ \sum_{q\ge q_{\rm s}} \frac{\|\ell_q-\ell_{3\lfloor q/3\rfloor}\|}{f(q)} &\le\varepsilon_p(L).\label{eq:charge-calibration-scalar} \end{aligned}$$ The function $\ell$ is nonnegative. If $0<p<1$, then for every collection $\mathcal E$ of E-step fluxes along a history, and every flag position $k$ below these steps, $$\label{eq:charge-scalar-tail} \sum_{\substack{q\in\mathcal E\\q\ge k}}(\ell_q)_+ \le \left(\frac{1+p}{3(1-p)}+\varepsilon_p(L)\right)f(k).$$ Changing $k$ by a fixed bounded amount, or taking $k$ in the terminal range, only changes $\varepsilon_p(L)$.
  - Explanation: Scalar corrections along flag and electron steps are controlled by the weight at the flag position, with errors vanishing as the scale grows.
- **claim-charge-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-charge-sums)): For either $p=1/8$ or $p=3/2$, as $L\to\infty$, $$\label{eq:charge-common-sums} \sup_{q\ge q_{\rm s}}\frac{B_q}{f(q)}=O(L^{-1}),\qquad \sum_{q\ge q_{\rm s}} \left(\frac{J_q}{f(q)}+\frac1{(L+q)^2}\right) =O\left(\frac{1+\log L}{L}\right).$$ For $p=1/8$ there are, in addition, $$\label{eq:charge-first-sums} \sum_{q\ge q_{\rm s}} \frac{\sup_{0\le m\le q}A_q(m)}{f(q)}=O(L^{-1}),\qquad \sum_{q\ge q_{\rm s}}\frac1{f(q)} \int_0^q\frac{A_q(m)}w\,dm=O(L^{-1/2}).$$ All implied constants are independent of $L$.
  - Explanation: The transported interaction sizes and related error sums become uniformly small as the weight scale grows.
- **claim-charge-calibration** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-charge-calibration)): For $q$ above the fixed stopping range, $$\label{eq:charge-calibrated-scalar} \left\|\ell_q+\langle K_q\rangle_{\chi_q}\right\| \le C\left(J_q+\|\ell_q-\ell_{q^0}\| +\frac{f(q)}{(L+q)^2}\right).$$ For any collection of northern center terms of $K_q$, their expectation in $\chi_q$ may instead be replaced by the linear reference functional of Theorem <a href="#thm:flags-local-readout" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:flags-local-readout">26</a> at child flux $q-3$; the additional error is at most $CJ_q$. This remains true when the choice between the two centerings is made separately on disjoint annuli.
  - Explanation: Calibrating an electron step against a filled step at the nearest lower flux divisible by three removes the accumulated scalar up to summable errors.
- **claim-weak-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-charge-weak-average)): Let $W$ be a neutral Hermitian interaction with bounded strength per unit area and fixed rapid locality bounds. For unit vectors $\psi\in Z_{n,q}$ and $\chi\in Z_{n',q}$ with hole numbers $h,h'$, $$\label{eq:charge-weak-average} \|\langle W\rangle_\psi-\langle W\rangle_\chi\| \le C_W(q+1)^{7/8}(h+h').$$ For radius-$r$ interactions of unit local norm one can take $C_W\le C(1+r)^B$ for a fixed $B$.
  - Explanation: After rotation averaging, expectations of a local interaction in two zero-mode states differ by a sublinear power of the flux times the total hole number.
- **claim-local-linear** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-charge-local-linear)): Let $q=3(N-1)$ and let $W$ be a neutral Hermitian interaction whose center density and rapid locality bounds are uniform in $q$. For every unit vector $\psi\in Z_{n,q}$, $$\label{eq:charge-local-linear} \bigl\|\langle W\rangle_\psi- \langle W\rangle_{\mathrm{Laughlin},N}\bigr\| \le C_W(N-n).$$ For interactions supported in radius $r$ with local norm at most one, $C_W$ is bounded by $C(1+r)^B$ for a fixed exponent $B$. The same conclusion holds for zero-mode density matrices by linearity, with the expected value of $N-\mathcal N$ on the right.
  - Explanation: A local interaction's expectation in any zero-mode vector differs from the filled Laughlin value by a constant times the electron deficit, with no growth in the area.
- **claim-energy-descent** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-local-energy-descent)): There are constants $c>0$, $L<\infty$, and $q_0$ such that, for every $q\ge q_0$, there is a measurable family of rapidly local physical operators $J_y$ on $\mathcal F_q$ satisfying $$\label{eq:energy-descent} \int J_y^*J_y\,d\nu_q(y)=H_q, \qquad \int J_y^*H_qJ_y\,d\nu_q(y)\le H_q^2-cH_q.$$ Every $J_y$ has a definite particle loss $r_y\in\{2,\ldots,L\}$, so that $[\mathcal N,J_y]=-r_yJ_y$. The center measure has bounded mass per unit ball, and all rapid-locality bounds are independent of $q$ and of the particle sector.
  - Explanation: A family of rapidly local particle-loss jumps resolves the interaction and strictly lowers interaction energy in the averaged sense, each jump removing a bounded number of particles.
- **claim-evacuation-rows** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-evacuation-rows)): There is a sequence $\varepsilon_M\to0$ such that, for every $M\ge2$ and every $q\ge30M$, $$\label{eq:evacuation-rows} \sum_{b=1}^{2q-1}\widetilde B_{b-1}^*\widetilde B_{b-1} \le\sum_{b>M}B_{b-1}^*B_{b-1}+\varepsilon_MI \quad\hbox{on }\mathcal F_q.$$
  - Explanation: After the local emptying channel the pair energy is bounded by that of the distant rows plus a vanishing error.
- **claim-pair-spin-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pair-spin-tail)): For $A_{q,M}$ in <a href="#eq:pair-tail-average" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pair-tail-average">[eq:pair-tail-average]</a>, there are coefficients $0\le a_{q,M,\ell}\le1$ such that $$A_{q,M}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{2q-2}a_{q,M,\ell}P_{q,\ell}.$$ For every fixed $\ell\ge0$, $$\label{eq:spin-tail-limit} \lim_{M\to\infty}\limsup_{q\to\infty}a_{q,M,\ell}=0.$$ Moreover, for each fixed $\ell$ there is $C_\ell<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:fixed-block-energy} \mathcal L_4(W_4P_{q,\ell}W_4^*)\le C_\ell H_q$$ for all sufficiently large $q$.
  - Explanation: The averaged truncated pair operator is diagonal in spin blocks, its weight on each fixed deficit vanishes in the limit, and each lifted block is bounded by the Hamiltonian.
- **claim-number-control** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-grand-canonical-coercivity)): There are constants $C_{\mathrm{num}}<\infty$, $\mu>0$, $a>0$, and $C_G<\infty$, independent of sufficiently large $q=3(N-1)$, such that $$\label{eq:excess-number-bound} \mathcal N-N\le C_{\mathrm{num}}H_q.$$ The operator $$\label{eq:coercive-G} G_q=H_q+\mu(N-\mathcal N)$$ satisfies $$\label{eq:G-coercivity} G_q\ge a\bigl(H_q+\|N-\mathcal N\|\bigr) \ge g\bigl(I-P_{\mathrm L,N}\bigr)$$ for a constant $g>0$, with $P_{\mathrm L,N}$ extended by zero on the other Fock sectors. For every initial density matrix, the evolution <a href="#eq:loss-generator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:loss-generator">[eq:loss-generator]</a> obeys $$\begin{aligned} \langle H_q\rangle_t&\le e^{-ct}\langle H_q\rangle_0, \label{eq:loss-energy-decay}\\ 0\le\langle\mathcal N\rangle_0-\langle\mathcal N\rangle_t &\le C_{\mathrm{num}}\langle H_q\rangle_0, \label{eq:loss-number-control}\\ \langle G_q\rangle_t&\le C_G\langle G_q\rangle_0. \label{eq:loss-G-control} \end{aligned}$$ Every limit point $\rho_\infty$ is supported on $\ker H_q$, and $$\label{eq:loss-hole-control} 0\le\operatorname{Tr}\rho_\infty(N-\mathcal N) \le C_G\mu^{-1}\langle G_q\rangle_0.$$
  - Explanation: Excess particle number is controlled by the interaction, the shifted Hamiltonian is coercive, and the loss evolution decays energy and controls number and hole count.
- **claim-relative-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-relative-bound)): For this fixed sufficiently large $\ell$, there is a constant $C$ independent of $q$ such that $$\label{eq:relative-bound} -CG\ \le\ \sum_x A_x\ \le\ CG$$ whenever the $A_x$ are physical, neutral, Hermitian operators satisfying $A_x\Omega=0$ and $\\|A_x\\|_{x,\ell}\le1$. If the sum or integral of localization norms in each cell is at most $S$, the conclusion becomes $-CSG\le\sum_xA_x\le CSG$.
  - Explanation: Sums of local terms annihilating the ground state are bounded above and below by a uniform multiple of the coercive operator, proved by a self-consistent quadratic inequality.
- **claim-retained-blocks** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-retained-blocks)): For every fixed $0<c_0<1/25$ there exists $q_0$ such that, for every integer $q\ge q_0$, the following inequality holds on the entire Fock space $\mathcal F_q$: $$\label{rb:eq:retained-blocks} H_q^2\ge c_0H_q+ \mathcal L_4\!\left(W_4R_q^{\mathrm{hi}}W_4^\dagger\right).$$ The threshold is independent of particle number.
  - Explanation: The square of the Hamiltonian dominates a small multiple of the Hamiltonian plus the lifted four-body blocks of high deficit, on the whole Fock space.
- **claim-normal-ordered-square** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-lem-square)): On $\mathcal F_q$, $$\label{rb:eq:square} H^2=H+\mathcal L_3\!\left(W_3(W_3^\dagger W_3-I)W_3^\dagger\right) +\mathcal L_4(W_4W_4^\dagger).$$
  - Explanation: Normal ordering writes the square of the Hamiltonian as the Hamiltonian plus an explicit three-body lift plus the full four-body lift.
- **claim-pair-coefficients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-lem-pair)): The coefficient of $e_i\wedge e_j$ in $v_p$, for $i<j$, vanishes unless $i+j=p+1$, and otherwise equals $$\label{rb:eq:pair} v_p(i,j)=(i-j)\sqrt{\frac{(q)_i(q)_j\,p!}{q(2q-2)_p\,i!j!}}.$$ For fixed $p,i,j$, its limit is $$\label{rb:eq:pairstar} v_p^*(i,j)=(i-j)\sqrt{\frac{p!}{2^p i!j!}}\,\mathbf1_{i+j=p+1}.$$
  - Explanation: Explicit binomial coefficients of the pair vectors and their fixed-index limits.
- **claim-three-body-gram** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-lem-q)): $$\label{rb:eq:q} W_3^\dagger W_3-I=\sum_{z=0}^q q_z(q)R_{3,z},\qquad q_z(q)=(-1)^z\frac{(q)_z}{(2q-2)_z} \left(3z-1-\frac{z(z+1)}q\right).$$ In particular $W_3R_{3,z}=0$ for $z=0,1,3$ whenever these indices are allowed. For fixed $z$, $$\label{rb:eq:qlimit} q_z(q)\longrightarrow (3z-1)(-1/2)^z.$$
  - Explanation: The Gram operator of the three-body wedge map is diagonal in spin blocks with explicit eigenvalues, vanishing on three blocks, with fixed-block limits.
- **claim-coupling-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-lem-coupling)): Suppose $j_1,j_2\to\infty$ and $j_2/(j_1+j_2)\to u^2$, where $0<u<1$. Put $v=\sqrt{1-u^2}$. For fixed integers $0\le z\le T$, the normalized coupled vector of spin $j_1+j_2-z$ at total lowering deficit $T$ can be chosen with real coefficients converging to the coefficients $s^{(u)}_{z,T,p}$ of $$\label{rb:eq:coupling} \frac{(uX-vY)^z(vX+uY)^{T-z}}{\sqrt{z!(T-z)!}}.$$ Descendants in every copy are obtained by normalized lowering, so the matching of bases between equal-spin copies is intertwining.
  - Explanation: Coupled vectors at fixed lowering deficit converge to explicit normalized polynomials in two oscillator variables.
- **claim-compression-lemma** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rb-lem-compression)): Let $W$ be a linear map between finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces, $G=W^\dagger W$, and let $C$ be self-adjoint on the domain of $W$. Then $$GCG\ge0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad WCW^\dagger\ge0.$$ On $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{loc}}$, for any fixed finite collection $A_b=c_kc_jB(v_p^*)$ with $0\le p\le23$ and $0\le j,k\le24$, $$\label{rb:eq:annihilatorbound} \sum_b\lVert A_b\psi\rVert^2\le C_0\langle \psi,h_*\psi\rangle, \qquad h_*:=\sum_{p=0}^{23}B(v_p^*)^\dagger B(v_p^*),$$ where $C_0$ depends only on the collection.
  - Explanation: Positivity of a form sandwiched by a Gram operator is equivalent to positivity after the map, and a finite family of annihilators is bounded by the pair energy.
- **claim-uniform-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-rb-lem-transfer)): For $q\ge24$ and $1\le D\le23$, put $$C_D^q=(d_D/d_a)\chi-E_D^q+\varepsilon I, \qquad h_q=\sum_{p=0}^{23}B_p^\dagger B_p.$$ Assuming <a href="#rb:eq:4certificate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rb:eq:4certificate">[rb:eq:4certificate]</a>, there is a scalar $\rho_q\ge0$, independent of $D$ and tending to zero as $q\to\infty$, such that $$\label{rb:eq:relative} \sum_{r,s\in\mathcal R_D}(C_D^q)_{rs} B(w_{Dr}^q)^\dagger B(w_{Ds}^q) \ge-\rho_qh_q$$ on the entire Fock space $\mathcal F_q$.
  - Explanation: The finite-flux four-body form is bounded below by minus a vanishing multiple of the local pair energy on the whole Fock space, by an exact diagonal change of variables.
- **claim-four-body-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-rb-prop-a4)): For the coefficients in Subsection <a href="#rb:app:certificate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rb:app:certificate">9.5</a>, $$\label{rb:eq:A4bound} A_4\le\mathcal L_4(W_4R_q^{\mathrm{lo}}W_4^\dagger)+(1222\varepsilon+o(1))H, \qquad q\to\infty,$$ uniformly on $\mathcal F_q$.
  - Explanation: The four-body part of the averaged comparison operator is dominated by the low-block target plus an explicit multiple of the Hamiltonian.
- **claim-three-body-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-rb-lem-tail)): For sufficiently large $q$, $$\label{rb:eq:tailbound} \mathcal L_3\left(W_3\sum_{z=16}^q q_z(q)R_{3,z}W_3^\dagger\right) \ge-\delta_qH,$$ where $$\label{rb:eq:delta} \delta_q=\sum_{\substack{17\le z\le q\\z\text{ odd}}} \frac{d_{3,z}}{d_a}\|q_z(q)\|(z+1),\qquad \delta_q\longrightarrow\frac{61}{4096}.$$
  - Explanation: The three-body blocks above the finitely checked range are bounded below by a multiple of the Hamiltonian with an explicit limiting coefficient.

Consequently, for each fixed $B\\geq0$, $$\\sum_{\\nu\\geq0}(C_*2^{\\nu+1}r)^B\\|A_\\nu\\|\n       \\leq C_Ba r^B.\n \\label{eq:local-core-moment}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-269.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "If <a href=\"#eq:local-core-tail\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:local-core-tail\">[eq:local-core-tail]</a> holds with $r\\geq1$, there is a decomposition $A=\\sum_{\\nu\\geq0}A_\\nu$ with $A_\\nu\\in\\mathfrak A(B_{C_*2^{\\nu+1}r}(x))$ and $\\|A_\\nu\\|\\leq C_Da2^{-\\nu D}$ for every $D$.         "anchor": "stmt-locality-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "The following bounds are uniform over the slot systems above.*\n\n1.  *Products of operators rapidly local at the same center, and adjoints, preserve rapid locality. If $A,B$ are centered at $x,y$, respectively, with bounded local norms, then $\\|[A,B]_{\\mathrm{gr}}\\|\\leq C_D(1+d(x,y))^{-D}$ for every $D$.*\n\n2.  *If $A$ is rapidly local at $x$ and a rapidly local interaction $K_y$ has tempered size $a(y)$, then $[\\int K_y\\,dy,A]_{\\mathrm{gr}}$ is rapidly local at $x$, of size $a(x)$ times the local bounds of $A$.*\n\n\n3.  *Suppose $A$ is even, $\\|A\\|_{x,l}\\leq1$, and $J_y$ has bounded rapid local norms. With $D=1+d(x,y)$ and $C_{xy}=[A,J_y]$, $$\\|C_{xy}\\|\\leq C D^{-l},\\qquad\n     \\|C_{xy}\\|_{x,l}\\leq C,\\qquad\n     \\|C_{xy}^*C_{xy}\\|_{x,l}\\leq C D^{-l}.\n     \\label{eq:commutator-square-locality}$$*\n\n*For an operator initially supported in $B_r(x)$, the corresponding core estimates cost a fixed polynomial in $r$. At distances larger than a fixed multiple of $r$, any prescribed decay order is available.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-locality-dynamics",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K$ be self-adjoint and even, with a rapidly local interaction of uniform size. Write $\\tau_t^K(A)=e^{itK}Ae^{-itK}$. For each $D$ there are $D'$ and $M_D$, independent of volume, such that $$\\|\\tau_t^K(A)\\|_{x,D}\n       \\leq C_D(1+|t|)^{M_D}\\|A\\|_{x,D'}.\n \\label{eq:polynomial-propagation}$$ The assertion holds also for time-dependent generators with the same uniform bounds. In particular evolution over a bounded parameter interval preserves rapid locality. If $A$ is supported in $B_r(x)$, the error in approximating $\\tau_t^K(A)$ on $B_R(x)$, for $R\\geq C(r+(1+|t|)^4)$, is bounded by $C_D\\|A\\|(1+r)^2R^{-D}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-locality-dynamics",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K$ be self-adjoint and even, with a rapidly local interaction of uniform size. Write $\\tau_t^K(A)=e^{itK}Ae^{-itK}$. In particular evolution over a bounded parameter interval preserves rapid locality. If $A$ is supported in $B_r(x)$, the error in approximating $\\tau_t^K(A)$ on $B_R(x)$, for $R\\geq C(r+(1+|t|)^4)$, is bounded by $C_D\\|A\\|(1+r)^2R^{-D}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-269.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $K$ be self-adjoint and even, with a rapidly local interaction of uniform size. Write $\\tau_t^K(A)=e^{itK}Ae^{-itK}$. In particular evolution over a bounded parameter interval preserves rapid locality.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-locality-filters",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $F\\in L^1(\\mathbb R)$ and $\\int(1+|t|)^j|F(t)|\\,dt<\\infty$ for every $j$. Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#locality:dynamics\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"locality:dynamics\">9</a>, $$\\mathcal T_{F,K}(A)=\\int_{\\mathbb R}F(t)\\tau_t^K(A)\\,dt\n \\label{eq:time-filter}$$ is rapidly local whenever $A$ is, with uniform bounds. The same is true for a finite measure consisting of such a function and a mass at zero. Number grade and physicality are preserved when $K$ is neutral and physical. Suitable symmetry of $F$ preserves adjoints.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

The same is true for a finite measure consisting of such a function and a mass at zero. Number grade and physicality are preserved when $K$ is neutral and physical. Suitable symmetry of $F$ preserves adjoints.",

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-locality-difference",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K,K'$ satisfy Lemma <a href=\"#locality:dynamics\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"locality:dynamics\">9</a> uniformly, and assume $K-K'$ has a rapidly local interaction of tempered size $a(z)$. For a rapidly local $A$ centered at $x$, $$\\|\\tau_t^K(A)-\\tau_t^{K'}(A)\\|_{x,D}\n \\leq C_Da(x)(1+|t|)^{M_D}\\|A\\|_{x,D'}.\n \\label{eq:weighted-evolution-difference}$$ The same conclusion holds after an all-moments time filter, and for differences of bounded-parameter evolutions. If the input itself changes by local size $b(x)$, its separate contribution is $Cb(x)$. All estimates retain the product of a local input size and $a(x)$. If $A\\in\\mathfrak A(B_r(x))$, put $s=\\kappa+2$, with $\\kappa$ from <a href=\"#eq:tempered-weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:tempered-weight\">[eq:tempered-weight]</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-locality-functional-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "The map <a href=\"#eq:physical-compression-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:physical-compression-map\">[eq:physical-compression-map]</a>, for the frames of Lemma <a href=\"#locality:frame\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"locality:frame\">4</a>, preserves rapid locality. An operator supported in $B_r(x)$ remains localized about that ball, with polynomial core costs and arbitrary inverse-power tails outside $B_{Cr}(x)$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-locality-row-symbols",
        "exact_quote": "With $m=b/2$ and $w=w_q(m)$, the zero-extended coefficients satisfy $$|\\Delta_i^r\\Delta_b^s a_b(i)|\n \\leq C_{D,r,s}w^{-1/2-r-s}\n       \\left(1+\\frac{|i-m|}{w}\\right)^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:pair-row-symbols}$$ There are pair annihilators $L_{\\nu,\\phi}$, rapidly local with uniform bounds about centers $x_{\\nu,\\phi}$, and a measure $d\\mu$ of bounded mass per unit ball, such that $$H_q=\\sum_\\nu\\int_0^{2\\pi}\n                L_{\\nu,\\phi}^*L_{\\nu,\\phi}\\,d\\mu_\\nu(\\phi).\n \\label{eq:pair-local-resolution}$$ The construction is linear in the row coefficients. In particular symbol bounds <a href=\"#eq:pair-row-symbols\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pair-row-symbols\">[eq:pair-row-symbols]</a> with an additional tempered factor give local bounds with that factor; the same holds for differences of row families in common frames.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-locality-row-symbols",
          "exact_quote": "With $m=b/2$ and $w=w_q(m)$, the zero-extended coefficients satisfy $$|\\Delta_i^r\\Delta_b^s a_b(i)|\n \\leq C_{D,r,s}w^{-1/2-r-s}\n       \\left(1+\\frac{|i-m|}{w}\\right)^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:pair-row-symbols}$$ There are pair annihilators $L_{\\nu,\\phi}$, rapidly local with uniform bounds about centers $x_{\\nu,\\phi}$, and a measure $d\\mu$ of bounded mass per unit ball, such that $$H_q=\\sum_\\nu\\int_0^{2\\pi}\n                L_{\\nu,\\phi}^*L_{\\nu,\\phi}\\,d\\mu_\\nu(\\phi).\n \\label{eq:pair-local-resolution}$$ The construction is linear in the row coefficients. In particular symbol bounds <a href=\"#eq:pair-row-symbols\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pair-row-symbols\">[eq:pair-row-symbols]</a> with an additional tempered factor give local bounds with that factor; the same holds for differences of row families in common frames.",
          "source_id": "PM-269.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "With $m=b/2$ and $w=w_q(m)$, the zero-extended coefficients satisfy $$|\\Delta_i^r\\Delta_b^s a_b(i)|\n \\leq C_{D,r,s}w^{-1/2-r-s}\n       \\left(1+\\frac{|i-m|}{w}\\right)^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:pair-row-symbols}$$ There are pair annihilators $L_{\\nu,\\phi}$, rapidly local with uniform bounds about centers $x_{\\nu,\\phi}$, and a measure $d\\mu$ of bounded mass per unit ball, such that $$H_q=\\sum_\\nu\\int_0^{2\\pi}\n                L_{\\nu,\\phi}^*L_{\\nu,\\phi}\\,d\\mu_\\nu(\\phi).\n \\label{eq:pair-local-resolution}$$ The construction is linear in the row coefficients. In particular symbol bounds <a href=\"#eq:pair-row-symbols\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pair-row-symbols\">[eq:pair-row-symbols]</a> with an additional tempered factor give local bounds with that factor; the same holds for differences of row families in common frames.",
      "explanation": "Pair coefficients obey scaled difference bounds, and the Hamiltonian is an integral of squares of rapidly local pair annihilators.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-locality-one-body-symbol",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $A(m)>0$ is tempered with exponent $\\kappa$ and $\\mathfrak s_K(a;m)\\leq C_KA(m)$ for every integer $K\\geq0$. Then $D_q(a)=\\sum_j a(j)c_j^*c_j$ has a physical, neutral, Hermitian interaction of size $A(m)$. For each output approximation order $D$, only the seminorms through $K_D=\\lceil4D+4\\kappa+40\\rceil$ are needed.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pinning-northern-observable",
        "exact_quote": "Let $O$ be a neutral operator of norm at most one supported on $B_r(x)$, where $r\\ge2$ and $x$ has latitude index $m$. There are fixed constants $b,C_*$, and $c=8$, independent of every tail order below, such that, if $q>Cr^b(1+m)$, the operator $$\\Delta_{q,d}(O)=U_{q,d}(1)^*OU_{q,d}(1)-O$$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\Delta_{q,d}(O)\\|&\\le C r^c\\frac{1+m}{q^2},\\label{eq:pinning-northern-norm}\\\\\n \\inf_{A\\in\\mathfrak A(B_{r+R}(x))}\\|\\Delta_{q,d}(O)-A\\|\n &\\le C_D r^c\\frac{1+m}{q^2}(1+R)^{-D}\n \\quad(R\\ge C_*(1+r)).\\label{eq:pinning-northern-tail}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, it admits a decomposition $$\\Delta_{q,d}(O)=\\sum_{\\nu\\ge0}D_\\nu,\\qquad\n D_\\nu\\in\\mathfrak A(B_{C_*2^\\nu r}(x)),\\qquad\n \\|D_\\nu\\|\\le C_D r^c\\frac{1+m}{q^2}2^{-\\nu D}.\n \\label{eq:pinning-northern-shells}$$ For $|q-q'|\\le3$, with the same $d$ and identical northern slots, $\\Delta_{q,d}(O)-\\Delta_{q',d}(O)$ obeys all these estimates with $q^{-2}$ replaced by $q^{-3}$. In particular, for every fixed $B$, the shell decomposition can be chosen so that $$\\sum_{\\nu\\ge0}(C_*2^\\nu r)^B\\|D_\\nu\\|\n \\le C_B r^{B+c}\\frac{1+m}{q^2},\n \\label{eq:pinning-northern-moment}$$ and the same moment estimate holds for the flux difference with $q^{-3}$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $q^0=3\\lfloor q/3\\rfloor$ and $r=q-q^0\\in\\{0,1,2\\}$. Consider the interaction $K_{q,3}(f)$ on $\\mathcal F_{q-3}$ and its common-frame comparison with $K_{q^0,3}(f)$. The following costs are each bounded by $$C\\int_0^{q-3}\\frac{A_q(m)}{v(m)}\\,dm:\n \\label{eq:pinning-calibration-cost}$$ the change of interaction in the embedded state, and the change of its expectation between the embedded and grown states. The same bounds hold after selecting any common collection of source-center pieces, and after adding their absolute bounds.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-flags-branch-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "For $q>q_{\\mathrm{stop}}$, define $$\\mathcal F_{n,q}=\\ker(c_q|_{Z_{n,q}}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal E_{n,q}=Z_{n,q}\\ominus\\mathcal F_{n,q}.$$ Then $\\mathcal F_{n,q}=Z_{n,q}^{(1)}$. Contraction by $c_q$ maps $\\mathcal E_{n,q}$ bijectively onto $Z_{n-1,q}^{(3)}$. If $\\mathsf T_q$ denotes the polar part of $c_q|_{Z_q}$, the maps $$J_{q,F}=\\mathcal U_{q,1},\\qquad\n J_{q,E}=\\mathsf T_q^*\\mathcal U_{q,3}$$ are isometries onto the two orthogonal branches. Consequently, $$Z_{n,q}\\simeq Z_{n,q-1}\\oplus Z_{n-1,q-3}.\n \\label{eq:flags-two-branches}$$ The second summand is absent for $n=0$. In the first summand $h$ decreases by one; in the second it is unchanged.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-flags-northern-step",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed $c\\geq0$ and $b_0\\geq1$ with the following property. Let $O=O^*$ be neutral, of norm at most one, supported on a slot ball of radius $r\\geq2$ centered at latitude index $m$, and put $u=1+m$. If $q>C r^{b_0}u$, identify all northern slots in the fluxes being compared. The two diagonal branch blocks of $O$ equal $$O+\\mathcal D_{q,1}(O)\\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad\n O+\\mathcal D_{q,3}(O)$$ up to a matrix remainder of norm $C_Dq^{-D}$ for every fixed $D$. Each $\\mathcal D_{q,d}(O)$ is neutral and self-adjoint. If $x$ is its original center, then, for fixed $c,C_*<\\infty$ and every $D>0$, $$\\begin{split}\n \\|\\mathcal D_{q,d}(O)\\|&\\leq Cr^cu/q^2,\\\\\n \\inf_{A_R\\in\\mathfrak A(B_R(x))}\n       \\|\\mathcal D_{q,d}(O)-A_R\\|\n  &\\leq C_Dr^cu q^{-2}R^{-D}\\qquad(R\\geq C_*r).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:flags-northern-size}$$ For $|q-q'|\\leq2$ and fixed $d$, the difference between these changes, in common northern slots, obeys the same two estimates with $q^{-2}$ replaced by $q^{-3}$. The exponent $c$ and core constant $C_*$ are independent of $D$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-flags-local-readout",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $a\\in(0,2)$ there are $B,C<\\infty$ such that the following holds uniformly in $n,q$ and in the allowed slot frames. Let $O=O^*$ be a neutral slot observable of norm at most one, supported in a ball of radius $r\\geq2$ with center of latitude index $m$. Its compression to $Z_{n,q}$ satisfies $$\\pm\\left(\n \\mathcal R_{n,q}P_{n,q}OP_{n,q}\\mathcal R_{n,q}^*\n       -\\operatorname{ref}_q(O)I\\right)\n \\leq C r^B W_m.\n \\label{eq:flags-local-readout}$$ Here $P_{n,q}$ denotes the zero-space projection in the number-$n$ sector. More generally, for a convergent decomposition $A=\\sum_\\nu A_\\nu$ into neutral self-adjoint observables supported in balls about the same center, of respective radii $r_\\nu\\geq2$, the right side can be replaced by $CW_m\\sum_\\nu r_\\nu^B\\|A_\\nu\\|$.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Let $O=O^*$ be a neutral slot observable of norm at most one, supported in a ball of radius $r\\geq2$ with center of latitude index $m$. Its compression to $Z_{n,q}$ satisfies $$\\pm\\left(\n \\mathcal R_{n,q}P_{n,q}OP_{n,q}\\mathcal R_{n,q}^*\n       -\\operatorname{ref}_q(O)I\\right)\n \\leq C r^B W_m.\n \\label{eq:flags-local-readout}$$ Here $P_{n,q}$ denotes the zero-space projection in the number-$n$ sector. More generally, for a convergent decomposition $A=\\sum_\\nu A_\\nu$ into neutral self-adjoint observables supported in balls about the same center, of respective radii $r_\\nu\\geq2$, the right side can be replaced by $CW_m\\sum_\\nu r_\\nu^B\\|A_\\nu\\|$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-flags-one-hole",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $h=1$, equivalently $q=3n-2$. Let $\\psi,\\phi$ be any two unit vectors in $Z_{n,q}$. Let $\\{O_x\\}$ be a measurable family of neutral self-adjoint observables of norm at most one, supported in balls of a fixed radius $r\\geq2$ about $x$, with center measure of bounded density. There are constants $C,B'<\\infty$, independent of $n,q$, such that $$\\int\\left|\n \\langle\\psi,O_x\\psi\\rangle-\\langle\\phi,O_x\\phi\\rangle\n \\right|\\,dx\\leq Cr^{B'}.\n \\label{eq:flags-one-hole}$$ The same conclusion holds for rapidly localized families, with the right side bounded by the corresponding fixed rapid-locality seminorm.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-charge-scalars",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $p\\in(0,2)$ and a lower stopping flux $q_{\\rm s}\\ge8$. There is a function $\\varepsilon_p(L)\\to0$ as $L\\to\\infty$ with the following properties, uniformly for $q\\ge q_{\\rm s}$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left|C_{q-1}(f)-C_q(f)+\\frac13f(q)\\right|\n &\\le\\varepsilon_p(L)f(q),\\label{eq:charge-F-scalar}\\\\\n \\sum_{q\\ge q_{\\rm s}}\n \\frac{|\\ell_q-\\ell_{3\\lfloor q/3\\rfloor}|}{f(q)}\n &\\le\\varepsilon_p(L).\\label{eq:charge-calibration-scalar}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The function $\\ell$ is nonnegative. If $0<p<1$, then for every collection $\\mathcal E$ of E-step fluxes along a history, and every flag position $k$ below these steps, $$\\label{eq:charge-scalar-tail}\n \\sum_{\\substack{q\\in\\mathcal E\\\\q\\ge k}}(\\ell_q)_+\n \\le \\left(\\frac{1+p}{3(1-p)}+\\varepsilon_p(L)\\right)f(k).$$ Changing $k$ by a fixed bounded amount, or taking $k$ in the terminal range, only changes $\\varepsilon_p(L)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-charge-scalars",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $p\\in(0,2)$ and a lower stopping flux $q_{\\rm s}\\ge8$. There is a function $\\varepsilon_p(L)\\to0$ as $L\\to\\infty$ with the following properties, uniformly for $q\\ge q_{\\rm s}$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left|C_{q-1}(f)-C_q(f)+\\frac13f(q)\\right|\n &\\le\\varepsilon_p(L)f(q),\\label{eq:charge-F-scalar}\\\\\n \\sum_{q\\ge q_{\\rm s}}\n \\frac{|\\ell_q-\\ell_{3\\lfloor q/3\\rfloor}|}{f(q)}\n &\\le\\varepsilon_p(L).\\label{eq:charge-calibration-scalar}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The function $\\ell$ is nonnegative. If $0<p<1$, then for every collection $\\mathcal E$ of E-step fluxes along a history, and every flag position $k$ below these steps, $$\\label{eq:charge-scalar-tail}\n \\sum_{\\substack{q\\in\\mathcal E\\\\q\\ge k}}(\\ell_q)_+\n \\le \\left(\\frac{1+p}{3(1-p)}+\\varepsilon_p(L)\\right)f(k).$$ Changing $k$ by a fixed bounded amount, or taking $k$ in the terminal range, only changes $\\varepsilon_p(L)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-269.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-charge-sums",
        "exact_quote": "For either $p=1/8$ or $p=3/2$, as $L\\to\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:charge-common-sums}\n \\sup_{q\\ge q_{\\rm s}}\\frac{B_q}{f(q)}=O(L^{-1}),\\qquad\n \\sum_{q\\ge q_{\\rm s}}\n \\left(\\frac{J_q}{f(q)}+\\frac1{(L+q)^2}\\right)\n =O\\left(\\frac{1+\\log L}{L}\\right).$$ For $p=1/8$ there are, in addition, $$\\label{eq:charge-first-sums}\n \\sum_{q\\ge q_{\\rm s}}\n \\frac{\\sup_{0\\le m\\le q}A_q(m)}{f(q)}=O(L^{-1}),\\qquad\n \\sum_{q\\ge q_{\\rm s}}\\frac1{f(q)}\n \\int_0^q\\frac{A_q(m)}w\\,dm=O(L^{-1/2}).$$ All implied constants are independent of $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-269.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-charge-weak-average",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a neutral Hermitian interaction with bounded strength per unit area and fixed rapid locality bounds.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-charge-local-linear",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q=3(N-1)$ and let $W$ be a neutral Hermitian interaction whose center density and rapid locality bounds are uniform in $q$. For every unit vector $\\psi\\in Z_{n,q}$, $$\\label{eq:charge-local-linear}\n \\bigl|\\langle W\\rangle_\\psi-\n          \\langle W\\rangle_{\\mathrm{Laughlin},N}\\bigr|\n \\le C_W(N-n).$$ For interactions supported in radius $r$ with local norm at most one, $C_W$ is bounded by $C(1+r)^B$ for a fixed exponent $B$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-local-energy-descent",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $c>0$, $L<\\infty$, and $q_0$ such that, for every $q\\ge q_0$, there is a measurable family of rapidly local physical operators $J_y$ on $\\mathcal F_q$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:energy-descent}\n \\int J_y^*J_y\\,d\\nu_q(y)=H_q,\n \\qquad\n \\int J_y^*H_qJ_y\\,d\\nu_q(y)\\le H_q^2-cH_q.$$ Every $J_y$ has a definite particle loss $r_y\\in\\{2,\\ldots,L\\}$, so that $[\\mathcal N,J_y]=-r_yJ_y$. The center measure has bounded mass per unit ball, and all rapid-locality bounds are independent of $q$ and of the particle sector.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-local-energy-descent",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $c>0$, $L<\\infty$, and $q_0$ such that, for every $q\\ge q_0$, there is a measurable family of rapidly local physical operators $J_y$ on $\\mathcal F_q$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:energy-descent}\n \\int J_y^*J_y\\,d\\nu_q(y)=H_q,\n \\qquad\n \\int J_y^*H_qJ_y\\,d\\nu_q(y)\\le H_q^2-cH_q.$$ Every $J_y$ has a definite particle loss $r_y\\in\\{2,\\ldots,L\\}$, so that $[\\mathcal N,J_y]=-r_yJ_y$. The center measure has bounded mass per unit ball, and all rapid-locality bounds are independent of $q$ and of the particle sector.",
          "source_id": "PM-269.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are constants $c>0$, $L<\\infty$, and $q_0$ such that, for every $q\\ge q_0$, there is a measurable family of rapidly local physical operators $J_y$ on $\\mathcal F_q$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:energy-descent}\n \\int J_y^*J_y\\,d\\nu_q(y)=H_q,\n \\qquad\n \\int J_y^*H_qJ_y\\,d\\nu_q(y)\\le H_q^2-cH_q.$$ Every $J_y$ has a definite particle loss $r_y\\in\\{2,\\ldots,L\\}$, so that $[\\mathcal N,J_y]=-r_yJ_y$. The center measure has bounded mass per unit ball, and all rapid-locality bounds are independent of $q$ and of the particle sector.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-evacuation-rows",
        "exact_quote": "There is a sequence $\\varepsilon_M\\to0$ such that, for every $M\\ge2$ and every $q\\ge30M$, $$\\label{eq:evacuation-rows}\n \\sum_{b=1}^{2q-1}\\widetilde B_{b-1}^*\\widetilde B_{b-1}\n \\le\\sum_{b>M}B_{b-1}^*B_{b-1}+\\varepsilon_MI\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathcal F_q.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pair-spin-tail",
        "exact_quote": "For $A_{q,M}$ in <a href=\"#eq:pair-tail-average\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pair-tail-average\">[eq:pair-tail-average]</a>, there are coefficients $0\\le a_{q,M,\\ell}\\le1$ such that $$A_{q,M}=\\sum_{\\ell=0}^{2q-2}a_{q,M,\\ell}P_{q,\\ell}.$$ For every fixed $\\ell\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:spin-tail-limit}\n \\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\limsup_{q\\to\\infty}a_{q,M,\\ell}=0.$$ Moreover, for each fixed $\\ell$ there is $C_\\ell<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{eq:fixed-block-energy}\n \\mathcal L_4(W_4P_{q,\\ell}W_4^*)\\le C_\\ell H_q$$ for all sufficiently large $q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pair-spin-tail",
          "exact_quote": "For $A_{q,M}$ in <a href=\"#eq:pair-tail-average\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pair-tail-average\">[eq:pair-tail-average]</a>, there are coefficients $0\\le a_{q,M,\\ell}\\le1$ such that $$A_{q,M}=\\sum_{\\ell=0}^{2q-2}a_{q,M,\\ell}P_{q,\\ell}.$$ For every fixed $\\ell\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:spin-tail-limit}\n \\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\limsup_{q\\to\\infty}a_{q,M,\\ell}=0.$$ Moreover, for each fixed $\\ell$ there is $C_\\ell<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{eq:fixed-block-energy}\n \\mathcal L_4(W_4P_{q,\\ell}W_4^*)\\le C_\\ell H_q$$ for all sufficiently large $q$.",
          "source_id": "PM-269.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $A_{q,M}$ in <a href=\"#eq:pair-tail-average\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pair-tail-average\">[eq:pair-tail-average]</a>, there are coefficients $0\\le a_{q,M,\\ell}\\le1$ such that $$A_{q,M}=\\sum_{\\ell=0}^{2q-2}a_{q,M,\\ell}P_{q,\\ell}.$$ For every fixed $\\ell\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:spin-tail-limit}\n \\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\limsup_{q\\to\\infty}a_{q,M,\\ell}=0.$$ Moreover, for each fixed $\\ell$ there is $C_\\ell<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{eq:fixed-block-energy}\n \\mathcal L_4(W_4P_{q,\\ell}W_4^*)\\le C_\\ell H_q$$ for all sufficiently large $q$.",
      "explanation": "The averaged truncated pair operator is diagonal in spin blocks, its weight on each fixed deficit vanishes in the limit, and each lifted block is bounded by the Hamiltonian.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-grand-canonical-coercivity",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $C_{\\mathrm{num}}<\\infty$, $\\mu>0$, $a>0$, and $C_G<\\infty$, independent of sufficiently large $q=3(N-1)$, such that $$\\label{eq:excess-number-bound}\n \\mathcal N-N\\le C_{\\mathrm{num}}H_q.$$ The operator $$\\label{eq:coercive-G}\n G_q=H_q+\\mu(N-\\mathcal N)$$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:G-coercivity}\n G_q\\ge a\\bigl(H_q+|N-\\mathcal N|\\bigr)\n \\ge g\\bigl(I-P_{\\mathrm L,N}\\bigr)$$ for a constant $g>0$, with $P_{\\mathrm L,N}$ extended by zero on the other Fock sectors. For every initial density matrix, the evolution <a href=\"#eq:loss-generator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:loss-generator\">[eq:loss-generator]</a> obeys $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\langle H_q\\rangle_t&\\le e^{-ct}\\langle H_q\\rangle_0,\n       \\label{eq:loss-energy-decay}\\\\\n 0\\le\\langle\\mathcal N\\rangle_0-\\langle\\mathcal N\\rangle_t\n    &\\le C_{\\mathrm{num}}\\langle H_q\\rangle_0,\n       \\label{eq:loss-number-control}\\\\\n \\langle G_q\\rangle_t&\\le C_G\\langle G_q\\rangle_0.\n       \\label{eq:loss-G-control}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Every limit point $\\rho_\\infty$ is supported on $\\ker H_q$, and $$\\label{eq:loss-hole-control}\n 0\\le\\operatorname{Tr}\\rho_\\infty(N-\\mathcal N)\n       \\le C_G\\mu^{-1}\\langle G_q\\rangle_0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-269.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-grand-canonical-coercivity",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $C_{\\mathrm{num}}<\\infty$, $\\mu>0$, $a>0$, and $C_G<\\infty$, independent of sufficiently large $q=3(N-1)$, such that $$\\label{eq:excess-number-bound}\n \\mathcal N-N\\le C_{\\mathrm{num}}H_q.$$ The operator $$\\label{eq:coercive-G}\n G_q=H_q+\\mu(N-\\mathcal N)$$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:G-coercivity}\n G_q\\ge a\\bigl(H_q+|N-\\mathcal N|\\bigr)\n \\ge g\\bigl(I-P_{\\mathrm L,N}\\bigr)$$ for a constant $g>0$, with $P_{\\mathrm L,N}$ extended by zero on the other Fock sectors. For every initial density matrix, the evolution <a href=\"#eq:loss-generator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:loss-generator\">[eq:loss-generator]</a> obeys $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\langle H_q\\rangle_t&\\le e^{-ct}\\langle H_q\\rangle_0,\n       \\label{eq:loss-energy-decay}\\\\\n 0\\le\\langle\\mathcal N\\rangle_0-\\langle\\mathcal N\\rangle_t\n    &\\le C_{\\mathrm{num}}\\langle H_q\\rangle_0,\n       \\label{eq:loss-number-control}\\\\\n \\langle G_q\\rangle_t&\\le C_G\\langle G_q\\rangle_0.\n       \\label{eq:loss-G-control}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Every limit point $\\rho_\\infty$ is supported on $\\ker H_q$, and $$\\label{eq:loss-hole-control}\n 0\\le\\operatorname{Tr}\\rho_\\infty(N-\\mathcal N)\n       \\le C_G\\mu^{-1}\\langle G_q\\rangle_0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-269.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are constants $C_{\\mathrm{num}}<\\infty$, $\\mu>0$, $a>0$, and $C_G<\\infty$, independent of sufficiently large $q=3(N-1)$, such that $$\\label{eq:excess-number-bound}\n \\mathcal N-N\\le C_{\\mathrm{num}}H_q.$$ The operator $$\\label{eq:coercive-G}\n G_q=H_q+\\mu(N-\\mathcal N)$$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:G-coercivity}\n G_q\\ge a\\bigl(H_q+|N-\\mathcal N|\\bigr)\n \\ge g\\bigl(I-P_{\\mathrm L,N}\\bigr)$$ for a constant $g>0$, with $P_{\\mathrm L,N}$ extended by zero on the other Fock sectors. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Laughlin flux and model sphere** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $N\ge2$, set $q=3(N-1)$ and let $S_q$ be the round sphere of radius $\sqrt{q/2}$.
- **Sphere S_q** ([source](#pm-h-10)): The sphere $S_q$ has radius $\sqrt{q/2}$, area $2\pi q$, and magnetic field one.
- **Lowest Landau level U_q** ([source](#pm-h-10)): The lowest Landau level $U_q$ is the spin-$q/2$ representation of $SU(2)$.
- **Coherent vector** ([source](#eq:toeplitz-resolution)): Write $k_x$ for the unit coherent vector at $x\in S_q$, defined up to a phase by the Riesz vector for evaluation at $x$.
- **Annihilation operator B(a)** ([source](#eq:lift)): For $a\in\bigwedge^k U_q$, let $B(a)$ be the adjoint of left wedge multiplication by $a$.
- **Spin summand of pairs** ([source](#eq:pair-rows)): Let $V_q\subset\bigwedge^2 U_q$ be the spin-$(q-1)$ summand.
- **Fock interaction H_q** ([source](#eq:fock-H)): On $n$ particles this is exactly $\sum_{i<j}P_{ij}^{(1)}$, with coefficient one for each unordered pair.
- **Zero space Z_{n,q}** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Put $Z_{n,q}=\ker H_{n,q}$ and let $P_{n,q}$ be its orthogonal projection.
- **Quasihole degree** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The number of flags is the *quasihole degree* $h=q+3-3n$.
- **Neutral operator** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Neutral means that a term preserves particle number
- **Rapidly local operator** ([source](#pm-h-15)): We call $A$ rapidly local at $x$ if this is finite for every $l$, with bounds uniform in the volume under consideration.
- **Tempered weight** ([source](#pm-h-15)): A positive function $a$ is called tempered if, for fixed $C,\kappa$,
- **Core radius and amplitude** ([source](#eq:local-core-tail)): A family has *core radius $r$ and amplitude $a$* if
- **Slot set** ([source](#eq:frame-atoms)): Write $\Lambda_q$ for this set of slots.

| q-unperturbed-gap-constant | coercivity constant gamma_* | = | $4616733319001/10^{14}$ | large flux, independent of particle number |
| q-filled-gap | gap of the filled-sector Hamiltonian | ≥ | $\frac1{25}$ | sufficiently large N |
| q-coherent-density | coherent-state resolution density | = | $\frac{q+1}{2\pi q}$ | every integer flux |
| q-laughlin-flux | Laughlin flux | = | $3(N-1)$ | all particle numbers at least two |
| q-quasihole-degree | degree of the symmetric factor | = | $q+3-3n$ | nonzero zero spaces |
| q-zero-space-dimension | dimension of the zero space | = | $\binom{n+h}{n}$ | nonnegative hole number |
| q-pinning-gap | gap along the pinning path | ≥ | $\gamma_{\mathrm{pin}}$ | large flux, both pinning depths, whole path |
| q-pinning-rate | local transport rate | = | $\frac{w^2}{v^2}$ | all latitudes |
| q-northern-bound | norm change of a northern observable under transport | ≤ | $C r^c\frac{1+m}{q^2}$ | northern region |
| q-northern-exponent | radius exponent in the northern bound | = | $8$ | northern region |
| q-occupation-gap | occupation gap of the south-pole orbital | = | $\delta/(\delta+8)$ | uniform in flux |
| q-retained-mass | retained mass of a pair row | ≥ | $\frac14$ | all fluxes in the argument |
| q-weak-average-bound | difference of averaged local expectations | ≤ | $C_W(q+1)^{7/8}(h+h')$ | neutral interactions with fixed rapid locality bounds |
| q-pooled-constant-first | pooled coefficient for the first exponent | = | $5/84$ | first weight exponent |
| q-pooled-constant-second | pooled coefficient for the summable exponent | = | $1/6$ | second weight exponent |
| q-annulus-flux-separation | annulus flux separation exponent | = | $\frac1{24}$ | summable weight |
| q-descent-constant | fixed coefficient used in the descent proof | = | $1/50$ | proof of local energy descent |
| q-maximal-loss | maximal particle loss of a jump | = | $3M+2$ | fixed cutoff |
| q-two-body-coefficient | two-body coefficient of the comparison operator | = | $\frac{93527408868499}{10^{14}}$ | fixed rational data |
| q-certificate-tolerance | tolerance in the four-body certificate | = | $3\cdot10^{-6}$ | spin blocks one through twenty-three |
| q-three-body-tail-limit | limit of the three-body tail coefficient | ~ | $\frac{61}{4096}$ | flux tending to infinity |
| q-four-body-allowance | coefficient of the Hamiltonian in the four-body estimate | ≤ | $1222\varepsilon+o(1)$ | flux tending to infinity |
| q-final-margin | limiting coefficient of the Hamiltonian after all comparisons | = | $\frac{4616733319001}{10^{14}}$ | flux tending to infinity |
